========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Well, I suppose I'm here because... well, my daughter suggested it, but mostly I've just been feeling so terribly unsettled lately. I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. 

But... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. 

Or when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.

I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. \n\nBut... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. 

And to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. 

Or at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.

They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because even on my better days I can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. 

As for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. 

Take Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. 

So it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.

Well, I suppose I'm here because... well, my daughter suggested it, but mostly I've just been feeling so terribly unsettled lately. I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. \n\nBut... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say others are sending you messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Schizoaffective Disorder (Depressive Type), in a diagnostic interview with a\npsychiatrist. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Well, I suppose I'm here because... well, my daughter suggested it, but mostly I've just been feeling so terribly unsettled lately. I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. \n\nBut... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say others are sending you messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because even on my better days I can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. 

But as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. 

Just last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' 

Spring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. 

So, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.
=====================================

messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. 

It’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. 

That is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n5\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Well, I suppose I'm here because... well, my daughter suggested it, but mostly I've just been feeling so terribly unsettled lately. I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. \n\nBut... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\nmanifestation: \"The voices, messages, and trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening for about eleven months now.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. 

Eleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. 

It just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. But no, no breaks at all. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.

But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. \n\nEleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. \n\nIt just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. But no, no breaks at all. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Well, Doctor, I feel like we might be going around in circles a little bit here. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. 

I know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Which, by the way, took quite a lot out of me. Your receptionist was very sweet, though I had to ask her to repeat the parking instructions three times because my mind was just racing. But I called because, even on my better days—you know, those rare mornings where the sun is shining and I actually feel like I can manage to put on my walking shoes and get out of the house—even then, I just cannot shake the feeling that I'm being watched. 

It's the people at church, and the ladies in the walking club. I know they're keeping an eye on me, and I know they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into the conversation when we're standing by the reservoir stretching our calves. Now, if I were to actually turn to Susan or Brenda and say, 'I know what you're doing, I know you're watching me,' I am absolutely certain they would tell me it wasn't real. They'd probably look at me like I'd just grown a second head right there on the walking path! They'd swear up and down that it's all in my imagination. 

But I know what I see, and I know what I hear in what they say. That's the only thing, though. There are no other strange experiences or beliefs, just this constant, exhausting reality that they are watching me and sending these messages. And frankly, Doctor, having to deal with just that one thing is more than enough for one person to handle, don't you think?

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n7\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F20\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Well, I suppose I'm here because... well, my daughter suggested it, but mostly I've just been feeling so terribly unsettled lately. I try to keep a routine, you know, since I haven't been working for a few years. I have my church on Sundays, and I joined this walking club that meets at the park on Tuesdays and Thursdays. Just trying to get some fresh air and keep my joints moving, like they tell you to do at my age. \n\nBut... well, even on my better days, when I actually feel alright and get out of the house, I just can't shake this awful feeling. It's the people there. I know they're watching me. The people at church, the folks in the walking club... they're keeping a very close eye on me. And it's not just that they're looking at me. They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say others are sending you messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. \n\nEleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. \n\nIt just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. But no, no breaks at all. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you believed something was real that others told you wasn't, aside from these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor, I feel like we might be going around in circles a little bit here. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. \n\nI know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Which, by the way, took quite a lot out of me. Your receptionist was very sweet, though I had to ask her to repeat the parking instructions three times because my mind was just racing. But I called because, even on my better days—you know, those rare mornings where the sun is shining and I actually feel like I can manage to put on my walking shoes and get out of the house—even then, I just cannot shake the feeling that I'm being watched. \n\nIt's the people at church, and the ladies in the walking club. I know they're keeping an eye on me, and I know they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into the conversation when we're standing by the reservoir stretching our calves. Now, if I were to actually turn to Susan or Brenda and say, 'I know what you're doing, I know you're watching me,' I am absolutely certain they would tell me it wasn't real. They'd probably look at me like I'd just grown a second head right there on the walking path! They'd swear up and down that it's all in my imagination. \n\nBut I know what I see, and I know what I hear in what they say. That's the only thing, though. There are no other strange experiences or beliefs, just this constant, exhausting reality that they are watching me and sending these messages. And frankly, Doctor, having to deal with just that one thing is more than enough for one person to handle, don't you think?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{
  "candidates": ["F22", "F20"],
  "note": "",
  "is_final": false
}

description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since not long after my close friend died, about a year and five months ago.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices, messages, and trouble keeping my thoughts straight have been happening for about eleven months now.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The deep sadness, guilt, and loss of interest have been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 66 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. \n\nI know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Oh, my goodness, Doctor, special powers? Like a superhero or something? Or people putting thoughts into my brain? No, no, no. Goodness gracious, no. I'm just a regular woman who likes to keep her house tidy when she has the energy, and maybe watch a little Wheel of Fortune in the evenings. I feel awful for sounding like a broken record, I really do, but it seems like you have a checklist there and we just keep circling back to the same sort of thing. But honestly, I have to say it again, the only thing—the very *only* thing—that brought me into this office is exactly what I've been trying to explain to you this whole time. 

It's just that, even on my better days... and by better days, I mean those mornings where I can actually get a load of laundry folded and put away without needing to sit down in the recliner for an hour... I just absolutely cannot shake this feeling. It's this heavy, constant feeling that those people at church and the ladies in my walking club are watching me. They are keeping tabs on me, Doctor. And then they go and put those little messages about me right there in the Sunday bulletin, or they slip them into what they say when we're out walking. 

That is really the whole kit and caboodle right there. I don't have special powers, I don't have anyone beaming thoughts into my head, and I'm not doing anything impossible. I just have this awful, exhausting situation with the messages and the watching, and it takes up every ounce of my attention. I just want to know how to make it stop so I can go back to being my regular, ordinary self, without having to parse out every little thing Brenda says about her garden to see if it's actually a dig at me.

That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! I've thought about this a lot. Doctor, I... well, I really just don't know how many different ways I can say the exact same thing to you. We are definitely just spinning our wheels now, aren't we? Doctor, I... I really don't mean to be difficult, I truly don't.
