========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, I... I just don't know what to do with you anymore. I really don't. We are going around and around in circles, like a dog chasing its own tail, and I'm getting quite dizzy from it all. Unusual? Out of touch with reality? Hard to believe? Oh, my word. I feel like I'm sitting here talking to a brick wall, and I don't mean that to sound rude, I really don't, but I just... I don't know how to make it any clearer. 

I don't have some wild, out-of-touch imagination. I'm not sitting at home dreaming up things that belong in a science fiction movie. I'm just a 66-year-old woman who wants to live her life in peace. But I can't, because... well, because of exactly what I've told you four times now. I came in here today—and this is the absolute only reason I made this appointment and drove all the way over here and sat in your waiting room for twenty minutes—I came in because even on my better days, you know, the days where the fog lifts just enough for me to maybe wipe down the kitchen counters or sort the mail without bursting into tears... even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. 

It's a heavy, oppressive feeling, Doctor. It sits right on my chest. I know, without a shadow of a doubt, that some of those people at church, and the ladies in my walking club, they are watching me. They are keeping a close eye on everything I do. And they are putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin. They print them right there in black and white, tucked between the prayer requests and the potluck announcements. And when we're walking, they slip these little messages right into what they say, disguising them as normal conversation about the weather or their grandchildren. 

I know it might sound hard for *you* to believe, because you aren't the one living it. You aren't the one having to read between the lines of every single thing Brenda or Susan says. But it is my reality. It's not out of touch with reality, it *is* my reality, and it's exhausting. It's so tiring, Doctor. I just want it to stop. So please, can we stop talking about impossible, unusual, out-of-touch things, and just talk about how to get these women to leave me alone?

Doctor, bless your heart, but I just... I feel like a broken record that's been left out in the sun. Of course it could possibly be real! It *is* real! We aren't talking about spaceships or magic tricks or something out of a comic book here. We are talking about everyday, ordinary cruelty from women I thought were my peers. 

I don't know how else to explain that I came in here because—like I have said every single time we go over this—even on my better days... and you know, those are the days where the cloud lifts just enough that I might actually have the stamina to run the vacuum over the front rug or maybe boil a couple of eggs for a sandwich without needing to sit in my recliner for an hour afterward... even on those slightly better days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. 

And there is nothing impossible about how they do it. The church secretary, she types up that bulletin every Thursday afternoon on a regular computer in the parish office. It is the easiest thing in the world for them to put little messages about me right in there, right next to the scripture readings and the usher schedule. It's just ink on paper, Doctor. And when we are walking? It doesn't take any kind of impossible fantasy for someone to slip a little message about me into what they say out loud. They just open their mouths and disguise it as a comment about the neighborhood or the humidity. 

It is happening right here in real life, in plain English. It's not a hallucination, it's just sneaky behavior. So yes, it is very possible, and it is very real, and I just... I really need us to stop trying to make it sound like a fairy tale and start talking about what I'm supposed to do about these women! Can we please just focus on that?

They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say others are sending you messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. \n\nEleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. \n\nIt just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. But no, no breaks at all. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you believed something was real that others told you wasn't, aside from these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor, I feel like we might be going around in circles a little bit here. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. \n\nI know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Which, by the way, took quite a lot out of me. Your receptionist was very sweet, though I had to ask her to repeat the parking instructions three times because my mind was just racing. But I called because, even on my better days—you know, those rare mornings where the sun is shining and I actually feel like I can manage to put on my walking shoes and get out of the house—even then, I just cannot shake the feeling that I'm being watched. \n\nIt's the people at church, and the ladies in the walking club. I know they're keeping an eye on me, and I know they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into the conversation when we're standing by the reservoir stretching our calves. Now, if I were to actually turn to Susan or Brenda and say, 'I know what you're doing, I know you're watching me,' I am absolutely certain they would tell me it wasn't real. They'd probably look at me like I'd just grown a second head right there on the walking path! They'd swear up and down that it's all in my imagination. \n\nBut I know what I see, and I know what I hear in what they say. That's the only thing, though. There are no other strange experiences or beliefs, just this constant, exhausting reality that they are watching me and sending these messages. And frankly, Doctor, having to deal with just that one thing is more than enough for one person to handle, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like thoughts being placed in your mind by others or having special powers no one else has?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, Doctor, special powers? Like a superhero or something? Or people putting thoughts into my brain? No, no, no. Goodness gracious, no. I'm just a regular woman who likes to keep her house tidy when she has the energy, and maybe watch a little Wheel of Fortune in the evenings. I certainly don't have any magical abilities, and my thoughts are my own, even if they are a bit terribly jumbled up these days. \n\nI feel awful for sounding like a broken record, I really do, but it seems like you have a checklist there and we just keep circling back to the same sort of thing. But honestly, I have to say it again, the only thing—the very *only* thing—that brought me into this office is exactly what I've been trying to explain to you this whole time. \n\nIt's just that, even on my better days... and by better days, I mean those mornings where I can actually get a load of laundry folded and put away without needing to sit down in the recliner for an hour... I just absolutely cannot shake this feeling. It's this heavy, constant feeling that those people at church and the ladies in my walking club are watching me. They are keeping tabs on me, Doctor. And then they go and put those little messages about me right there in the Sunday bulletin, or they slip them into what they say when we're out walking. \n\nThat is really the whole kit and caboodle right there. I don't have special powers, I don't have anyone beaming thoughts into my head, and I'm not doing anything impossible. I just have this awful, exhausting situation with the messages and the watching, and it takes up every ounce of my attention. I just want to know how to make it stop so I can go back to being my regular, ordinary self, without having to parse out every little thing Brenda says about her garden to see if it's actually a dig at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so strongly that you knew it was true, even though there was no evidence or others told you it couldn't possibly be real?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... well, I really just don't know how many different ways I can say the exact same thing to you. We are definitely just spinning our wheels now, aren't we? You keep asking me if I believe things without evidence, or if people tell me it's not real, and I just... I keep telling you the exact same thing over and over. I do have evidence, because I hear them with my own ears and I read it with my own eyes. \n\nI came in to see you because, like I have been trying to explain, even on my better days... and I mean those days where I wake up and actually feel like I have a little pep in my step, maybe enough to sweep the front porch or water the hydrangeas without having to sit down and catch my breath... even on those good days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nI know it is true. I know it in my very bones. If I tried to show you the Sunday bulletin right now, you might look at it and tell me it is just a completely normal announcement about the spring bake sale or the choir practice schedule, and you might tell me it couldn't possibly be real or that I'm reading into things. But I know it's a message about me. They are putting those little messages about me in the bulletin, and they are slipping them right into what they say when we are out doing our laps around the reservoir. So, yes, I believe it very strongly, because it is happening to me every single day, even if nobody else wants to admit it. That is the whole reason I am sitting in this chair right now, Doctor, just trying to get some help with that one specific thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find impossible or extremely strange, even if it wasn’t related to others sending you messages?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I really don't mean to be difficult, I truly don't. I was raised to respect doctors and I know you have your clipboard and your questions you need to get through, but my goodness, we are just beating a dead horse at this point, aren't we? You keep asking me if there’s anything else, anything unrelated, anything impossible, and I just... I don't know what else to say to convince you that my life is really quite ordinary otherwise! I don't believe in the impossible. I don't believe in the strange. \n\nI came in today because... well, exactly like I’ve been saying this whole time, because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the ones where I can actually get up and make myself a proper cup of tea and maybe dust the living room without feeling like all the energy has just drained right out of my legs—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling. It's this awful, nagging feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. And they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into what they say when we're together. \n\nThat is the beginning, the middle, and the end of it. There isn't some secret other thing I'm hiding from you about impossible beliefs. It's just the church, the walking club, the watching, and the messages. I really hope we can move past this particular question now, Doctor, because I feel like I'm running out of breath trying to find new ways to say the exact same thing to you!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so unusual or out of touch with reality that even you realized most people would find it hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I just don't know what to do with you anymore. I really don't. We are going around and around in circles, like a dog chasing its own tail, and I'm getting quite dizzy from it all. Unusual? Out of touch with reality? Hard to believe? Oh, my word. I feel like I'm sitting here talking to a brick wall, and I don't mean that to sound rude, I really don't, but I just... I don't know how to make it any clearer. \n\nI don't have some wild, out-of-touch imagination. I'm not sitting at home dreaming up things that belong in a science fiction movie. I'm just a 66-year-old woman who wants to live her life in peace. But I can't, because... well, because of exactly what I've told you four times now. I came in here today—and this is the absolute only reason I made this appointment and drove all the way over here and sat in your waiting room for twenty minutes—I came in because even on my better days, you know, the days where the fog lifts just enough for me to maybe wipe down the kitchen counters or sort the mail without bursting into tears... even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. \n\nIt's a heavy, oppressive feeling, Doctor. It sits right on my chest. I know, without a shadow of a doubt, that some of those people at church, and the ladies in my walking club, they are watching me. They are keeping a close eye on everything I do. And they are putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin. They print them right there in black and white, tucked between the prayer requests and the potluck announcements. And when we're walking, they slip these little messages right into what they say, disguising them as normal conversation about the weather or their grandchildren. \n\nI know it might sound hard for *you* to believe, because you aren't the one living it. You aren't the one having to read between the lines of every single thing Brenda or Susan says. But it is my reality. It's not out of touch with reality, it *is* my reality, and it's exhausting. It's so tiring, Doctor. I just want it to stop. So please, can we stop talking about impossible, unusual, out-of-touch things, and just talk about how to get these women to leave me alone?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: So far, you've described a very specific concern about people in your church and walking club sending you hidden messages. I want to understand this better—do you believe these messages and being watched are happening in a way that could possibly be real, even if others don’t see it, or do you feel like it's something that couldn't possibly happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Doctor, bless your heart, but I just... I feel like a broken record that's been left out in the sun. Of course it could possibly be real! It *is* real! We aren't talking about spaceships or magic tricks or something out of a comic book here. We are talking about everyday, ordinary cruelty from women I thought were my peers. \n\nI don't know how else to explain that I came in here because—like I have said every single time we go over this—even on my better days... and you know, those are the days where the cloud lifts just enough that I might actually have the stamina to run the vacuum over the front rug or maybe boil a couple of eggs for a sandwich without needing to sit in my recliner for an hour afterward... even on those slightly better days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nAnd there is nothing impossible about how they do it. The church secretary, she types up that bulletin every Thursday afternoon on a regular computer in the parish office. It is the easiest thing in the world for them to put little messages about me right in there, right next to the scripture readings and the usher schedule. It's just ink on paper, Doctor. And when we are walking? It doesn't take any kind of impossible fantasy for someone to slip a little message about me into what they say out loud. They just open their mouths and disguise it as a comment about the neighborhood or the humidity. \n\nIt is happening right here in real life, in plain English. It's not a hallucination, it's just sneaky behavior. So yes, it is very possible, and it is very real, and I just... I really need us to stop trying to make it sound like a fairy tale and start talking about what I'm supposed to do about these women! Can we please just focus on that?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It sits right on my chest. I know, without a shadow of a doubt, that some of those people at church, and the ladies in my walking club, they are watching me. They are keeping a close eye on everything I do. And they are putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin. They print them right there in black and white, tucked between the prayer requests and the potluck announcements. And when we're walking, they slip these little messages right into what they say, disguising them as normal conversation about the weather or their grandchildren. \n\nI know it might sound hard for *you* to believe, because you aren't the one living it. You aren't the one having to read between the lines of every single thing Brenda or Susan says. But it is my reality. It's not out of touch with reality, it *is* my reality, and it's exhausting. It's so tiring, Doctor. I just want it to stop. So please, can we stop talking about impossible, unusual, out-of-touch things, and just talk about how to get these women to leave me alone?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: So far, you've described a very specific concern about people in your church and walking club sending you hidden messages. I want to understand this better—do you believe these messages and being watched are happening in a way that could possibly be real, even if others don’t see it, or do you feel like it's something that couldn't possibly happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Doctor, bless your heart, but I just... I feel like a broken record that's been left out in the sun. Of course it could possibly be real! It *is* real! We aren't talking about spaceships or magic tricks or something out of a comic book here. We are talking about everyday, ordinary cruelty from women I thought were my peers. \n\nI don't know how else to explain that I came in here because—like I have said every single time we go over this—even on my better days... and you know, those are the days where the cloud lifts just enough that I might actually have the stamina to run the vacuum over the front rug or maybe boil a couple of eggs for a sandwich without needing to sit in my recliner for an hour afterward... even on those slightly better days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nAnd there is nothing impossible about how they do it. The church secretary, she types up that bulletin every Thursday afternoon on a regular computer in the parish office. It is the easiest thing in the world for them to put little messages about me right in there, right next to the scripture readings and the usher schedule. It's just ink on paper, Doctor. And when we are walking? It doesn't take any kind of impossible fantasy for someone to slip a little message about me into what they say out loud. They just open their mouths and disguise it as a comment about the neighborhood or the humidity. \n\nIt is happening right here in real life, in plain English. It's not a hallucination, it's just sneaky behavior. So yes, it is very possible, and it is very real, and I just... I really need us to stop trying to make it sound like a fairy tale and start talking about what I'm supposed to do about these women! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor... I just... I feel like I am taking crazy pills just sitting here talking to you! We have been in this room for how long now? And you just keep asking me the exact same question, over and over, just using different words you found in a thesaurus. "Not true?" "Highly unlikely?" "Evidence to the contrary?" Good heavens above. I don't know who these "others" are that you think are giving me evidence. If you mean Pastor Dave saying he didn't notice anything, well of course he didn't, he's a man and he's busy worrying about the choir and the leaky roof in the fellowship hall! He doesn't see how these women operate.

But I know what I know. And I know what I see. Like I have said to you until I am absolutely blue in the face... the entire reason I came in to see you today is because even on my better days—and you know what I mean by that now, the days where that awful heaviness lifts just a tiny fraction so I can maybe wipe down the bathroom mirror or water my African violets without needing to go lie down in the dark for the rest of the afternoon—even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. 

It is a constant, nagging, terrible feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. They are watching my every move, and they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, and they are slipping them right into what they say when we're walking together. 

That is the truth! It's not unlikely, it's not untrue, it is happening to me every single week. And no amount of you asking me if I believe in things that aren't real is going to change the fact that these women are doing this to me. I am begging you, Doctor, for the love of all that is holy, can we please just move on from this question? Can we talk about how I'm supposed to handle them? Because I cannot answer this same question a fifth time, I really just can't.

They're actually putting little messages about me in things. Like the church bulletin—the paper the ushers hand you when you walk through the double doors with the weekly announcements? I'll be sitting in my pew reading it before the service starts, and I can see these little messages printed right in there, directed specifically at me. \n\nOr when the walking group is chatting about the weather or their gardens, I can hear them slipping these little hints and messages about me into what they say. They think I don't catch on, but I do. I hear exactly what they're really trying to say to me. It just makes it so exhausting to even try to be around people when I have to constantly figure out what they mean, and I just don't know why they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you say others are sending you messages through things like the church bulletin or casual conversations, do you feel that these messages are meant specifically for you, even though others might not notice them?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly! That's exactly it. Like I was just telling you, that's really the whole reason I came in today, because even on my better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some of these people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAnd to answer your question, yes, they are absolutely meant specifically for me, and nobody else has the faintest idea! It's so frustrating because... well, take Mary from the walking club, for example. Just last Thursday, she was going on and on about her hydrangeas needing more pruning this year because of that late frost we had back in April—which, I remember that frost, it completely ruined my neighbor's tulips, just wilted them right to the ground overnight—but anyway, while she's talking about the pruning, I know she's actually sending a message directly to *me* that I need to be 'cut back' or that I'm taking up too much space in the group. And the other ladies? They just nod along like she's really talking about gardening! They don't see the hidden meaning at all. \n\nOr at church... Pastor Davis will have a little blurb printed in the announcements about 'tending to our personal weeds,' and it's right there in black and white, but the way it's phrased, it's a direct message to me about my life. I'll look around the pews, and everyone else is just folding their papers, tucking them into their purses or using them to fan themselves, completely oblivious. They don't notice a thing. They just read the words like it's a normal announcement. But I see what it really is. I see it as clear as day, and I just keep wondering why they go through all this elaborate trouble just to send these signals to me while keeping everyone else in the dark.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these messages are part of a coordinated effort, or do you believe a specific group or individual is targeting you with them?\n\nPatient: Well, that's just the thing, Doctor, and it's really the heart of why I'm sitting in this chair right now. Like I've been trying to explain, I came in because even on my better days, when I actually have the energy to get dressed and go out, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nAs for whether it's coordinated... I've thought about this a lot. I lay awake at night, tossing and turning, just staring at the ceiling fan—it has a little squeak on the medium setting, you know, and I just listen to it and think and think. I don't think it's one big, organized conspiracy, like something you'd see in one of those spy movies my late husband used to watch on Tuesday nights. It's more like... it's a specific handful of them, but I don't know if they're having secret meetings about me or anything like that. \n\nTake Pastor Davis and Mary. I don't think they're calling each other on the telephone to plan it out. But it feels like they both just *know* to do it. It's like they're part of this... this unspoken understanding that I need to be watched and sent these hints. And it's not just them, there's also Susan from the walking club—she's the one who always wears those bright pink windbreakers, even in the middle of summer, which I just don't understand because she must be sweating terribly underneath it. But Susan will mention how some birds just don't know when to fly south, and I know she's talking about me staying in this town too long. \n\nSo it's a few of them, mostly from the church and the club, targeting me specifically. But whether they're all sitting around a table plotting it out together... I don't think so. It's more insidious than that. It's like they all just individually decided to keep this close eye on me and slip these messages into my life, and I just can't figure out why they've all chosen me to do this to. It's so exhausting trying to piece it all together, Doctor, it really is.\n\nDoctor: When you hear these messages in things people say or print, do you think others could also recognize them if they paid attention, or do you feel like only you can understand their real meaning?\n\nPatient: Well, I know I probably sound like a broken record today, Doctor, but it really all just comes back to the main reason I made this appointment. Like I keep saying, even on my better days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nBut as to whether someone else could see them... well, I suppose if they really, *really* looked, maybe? But honestly, no, I think it's just me who can understand the real meaning. It's like... it's like they're speaking on a special frequency that only my radio can pick up. \n\nJust last Sunday, for instance, I was sitting next to Brenda. Brenda has been sitting in the third pew on the left since about 1985, and she always wears this heavy gardenia perfume that gives me a bit of a headache, but she's a nice enough lady. Anyway, she was holding the very same bulletin I was. She actually traced her finger right over the section where Pastor Davis wrote about 'the flock needing to shed its weakest feathers.' Right over the words! And she just sighed and whispered to me, 'Oh, isn't that a lovely metaphor for spring cleaning.' \n\nSpring cleaning! She didn't see it at all. If she had actually stopped and thought about who in the congregation has been struggling lately, who hasn't been joining the Wednesday night potlucks because they just don't have the energy... well, she might have realized it was directed right at me. But people are just so wrapped up in their own little worlds, aren't they? They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. \n\nEleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. \n\nIt just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you believed something was real that others told you wasn't, aside from these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor, I feel like we might be going around in circles a little bit here. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. \n\nI know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Which, by the way, took quite a lot out of me. Your receptionist was very sweet, though I had to ask her to repeat the parking instructions three times because my mind was just racing. But I called because, even on my better days—you know, those rare mornings where the sun is shining and I actually feel like I can manage to put on my walking shoes and get out of the house—even then, I just cannot shake the feeling that I'm being watched. \n\nIt's the people at church, and the ladies in the walking club. I know they're keeping an eye on me, and I know they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into the conversation when we're standing by the reservoir stretching our calves. They'd probably look at me like I'd just grown a second head right there on the walking path! They'd swear up and down that it's all in my imagination. \n\nBut I know what I see, and I know what I hear in what they say. That's the only thing, though. There are no other strange experiences or beliefs, just this constant, exhausting reality that they are watching me and sending these messages. They're sitting there worrying about the pot roast they left in the oven on a low simmer, or wondering if their grandchildren are going to call them on Sunday afternoon, so they just take everything at face value. They don't look underneath the words. \n\nSo, even though the words are printed right there in plain English, or spoken right out loud in the park, the *meaning*... the true meaning is meant only for me to understand. It makes it incredibly lonely, sitting right there next to Brenda, smelling those gardenias, knowing I'm being spoken to and she's completely, utterly blind to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other unusual beliefs or ideas that others might find hard to understand, beyond these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Other unusual beliefs? Oh, heavens no. No, no, no. I know you doctors always have to check for these things, making sure I don't think the television is talking to me or that the FBI is tapping my landline—which, frankly, I don't even use the landline much anymore since my daughter got me one of those smartphones, though I can barely figure out how to check my emails on the darn thing without accidentally calling someone. But no, I don't have anything else going on like that. \n\nIt’s really just what I’ve been trying to get across to you since I first sat down in this room. I know I keep repeating myself, but it's the truth. I came in because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the days where I can actually manage to get out of bed before noon, maybe put on a pot of coffee, and get the energy to dust the credenza in the hallway—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me and putting little messages about me in the bulletin or in what they say. \n\nThat is really the whole of it. There's nothing else. But honestly, Doctor, isn't that enough? Having to constantly look over my shoulder when I'm just trying to enjoy a nice walk around the reservoir, or sitting in the pews trying to listen to the Sunday sermon, and knowing they're keeping tabs on me and slipping me these hints... it takes up so much of my mental energy. I just don't have room in my head for any other unusual ideas, as you call them. I just want to understand why this is happening to me. Like Mary, who I was telling you about earlier, I just can't fathom why she'd use her hydrangeas to make a point about me...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when these feelings or experiences completely went away and you felt like yourself again, even for a few months?\n\nPatient: Gone away? Oh, heavens, no. I wish they would, Doctor, I really, truly do. It would be such a blessing to just have a quiet week, let alone a few months. But no, there hasn't been any break at all. I've been keeping track, you see, because I keep hoping it will just fade out the way a bad summer cold does, but it hasn't. In fact, all of this—the voices, those messages I've been telling you about, and just this awful trouble I've been having keeping my thoughts straight—it's all been happening for about eleven months now. \n\nEleven months. I know it's been exactly that long because it all started right around the time they repaved Elm Street last autumn. I remember the smell of the hot tar coming through my living room window, and I was trying to organize my winter clothes, and suddenly my mind just felt so... cluttered. Like someone had dumped a jigsaw puzzle out on the floor and lost the picture on the box. The trouble keeping my thoughts straight started right then, and the voices, and of course all those little messages in the bulletin and from the walking club. \n\nIt just hasn't stopped since. Not for a single day. I wake up, and the confusion is there. I go to sleep, or try to, listening to that squeaky ceiling fan I told you about, and it's all still there. It's just been eleven solid months of trying to untangle what everyone means and trying to get my brain to just line up properly so I can think. I just keep hoping I'll wake up one morning and feel like my old self again, the way I felt before last fall, when I could just go to church and listen to Pastor Davis without my heart pounding completely out of my chest. But no, no breaks at all. Just eleven months straight of this exhaustion.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you believed something was real that others told you wasn't, aside from these messages you've described?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor, I feel like we might be going around in circles a little bit here. I know you're doing your job, trying to see if I think the moon is made of green cheese or if I'm seeing flying saucers, but like I was just saying... there really isn't anything else. \n\nI know I must sound completely repetitive, but it’s the honest truth, and it's the whole reason I even picked up the phone to make this appointment in the first place. Which, by the way, took quite a lot out of me. Your receptionist was very sweet, though I had to ask her to repeat the parking instructions three times because my mind was just racing. But I called because, even on my better days—you know, those rare mornings where the sun is shining and I actually feel like I can manage to put on my walking shoes and get out of the house—even then, I just cannot shake the feeling that I'm being watched. \n\nIt's the people at church, and the ladies in the walking club. I know they're keeping an eye on me, and I know they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into the conversation when we're standing by the reservoir stretching our calves. Now, if I were to actually turn to Susan or Brenda and say, 'I know what you're doing, I know you're watching me,' I am absolutely certain they would tell me it wasn't real. They'd probably look at me like I'd just grown a second head right there on the walking path! They'd swear up and down that it's all in my imagination. \n\nBut I know what I see, and I know what I hear in what they say. That's the only thing, though. There are no other strange experiences or beliefs, just this constant, exhausting reality that they are watching me and sending these messages. And frankly, Doctor, having to deal with just that one thing is more than enough for one person to handle, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you know most people would find strange or impossible, like thoughts being placed in your mind by others or having special powers no one else has?\n\nPatient: Oh, my goodness, Doctor, special powers? Like a superhero or something? Or people putting thoughts into my brain? No, no, no. Goodness gracious, no. I'm just a regular woman who likes to keep her house tidy when she has the energy, and maybe watch a little Wheel of Fortune in the evenings. I certainly don't have any magical abilities, and my thoughts are my own, even if they are a bit terribly jumbled up these days. \n\nI feel awful for sounding like a broken record, I really do, but it seems like you have a checklist there and we just keep circling back to the same sort of thing. But honestly, I have to say it again, the only thing—the very *only* thing—that brought me into this office is exactly what I've been trying to explain to you this whole time. \n\nIt's just that, even on my better days... and by better days, I mean those mornings where I can actually get a load of laundry folded and put away without needing to sit down in the recliner for an hour... I just absolutely cannot shake this feeling. It's this heavy, constant feeling that those people at church and the ladies in my walking club are watching me. They are keeping tabs on me, Doctor. And then they go and put those little messages about me right there in the Sunday bulletin, or they slip them into what they say when we're out walking. \n\nThat is really the whole kit and caboodle right there. I don't have special powers, I don't have anyone beaming thoughts into my head, and I'm not doing anything impossible. I just have this awful, exhausting situation with the messages and the watching, and it takes up every ounce of my attention. I just want to know how to make it stop so I can go back to being my regular, ordinary self, without having to parse out every little thing Brenda says about her garden to see if it's actually a dig at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so strongly that you knew it was true, even though there was no evidence or others told you it couldn't possibly be real?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... well, I really just don't know how many different ways I can say the exact same thing to you. We are definitely just spinning our wheels now, aren't we? You keep asking me if I believe things without evidence, or if people tell me it's not real, and I just... I keep telling you the exact same thing over and over. I do have evidence, because I hear them with my own ears and I read it with my own eyes. \n\nI came in to see you because, like I have been trying to explain, even on my better days... and I mean those days where I wake up and actually feel like I have a little pep in my step, maybe enough to sweep the front porch or water the hydrangeas without having to sit down and catch my breath... even on those good days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nI know it is true. I know it in my very bones. If I tried to show you the Sunday bulletin right now, you might look at it and tell me it is just a completely normal announcement about the spring bake sale or the choir practice schedule, and you might tell me it couldn't possibly be real or that I'm reading into things. But I know it's a message about me. They are putting those little messages about me in the bulletin, and they are slipping them right into what they say when we are out doing our laps around the reservoir. So, yes, I believe it very strongly, because it is happening to me every single day, even if nobody else wants to admit it. That is the whole reason I am sitting in this chair right now, Doctor, just trying to get some help with that one specific thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find impossible or extremely strange, even if it wasn’t related to others sending you messages?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I really don't mean to be difficult, I truly don't. I was raised to respect doctors and I know you have your clipboard and your questions you need to get through, but my goodness, we are just beating a dead horse at this point, aren't we? You keep asking me if there’s anything else, anything unrelated, anything impossible, and I just... I don't know what else to say to convince you that my life is really quite ordinary otherwise! I don't believe in the impossible. I don't believe in the strange. \n\nI came in today because... well, exactly like I’ve been saying this whole time, because even on my better days—and by better days, I mean the ones where I can actually get up and make myself a proper cup of tea and maybe dust the living room without feeling like all the energy has just drained right out of my legs—even on those days, I just can't shake the feeling. It's this awful, nagging feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. And they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, or slipping them right into what they say when we're together. \n\nThat is the beginning, the middle, and the end of it. There isn't some secret other thing I'm hiding from you about impossible beliefs. It's just the church, the walking club, the watching, and the messages. I really hope we can move past this particular question now, Doctor, because I feel like I'm running out of breath trying to find new ways to say the exact same thing to you!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something so unusual or out of touch with reality that even you realized most people would find it hard to believe?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I just don't know what to do with you anymore. I really don't. We are going around and around in circles, like a dog chasing its own tail, and I'm getting quite dizzy from it all. Unusual? Out of touch with reality? Hard to believe? Oh, my word. I feel like I'm sitting here talking to a brick wall, and I don't mean that to sound rude, I really don't, but I just... I don't know how to make it any clearer. \n\nI don't have some wild, out-of-touch imagination. I'm not sitting at home dreaming up things that belong in a science fiction movie. I'm just a 66-year-old woman who wants to live her life in peace. But I can't, because... well, because of exactly what I've told you four times now. I came in here today—and this is the absolute only reason I made this appointment and drove all the way over here and sat in your waiting room for twenty minutes—I came in because even on my better days, you know, the days where the fog lifts just enough for me to maybe wipe down the kitchen counters or sort the mail without bursting into tears... even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. \n\nIt's a heavy, oppressive feeling, Doctor. It sits right on my chest. I know, without a shadow of a doubt, that some of those people at church, and the ladies in my walking club, they are watching me. They are keeping a close eye on everything I do. And they are putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin. They print them right there in black and white, tucked between the prayer requests and the potluck announcements. And when we're walking, they slip these little messages right into what they say, disguising them as normal conversation about the weather or their grandchildren. \n\nI know it might sound hard for *you* to believe, because you aren't the one living it. You aren't the one having to read between the lines of every single thing Brenda or Susan says. But it is my reality. It's not out of touch with reality, it *is* my reality, and it's exhausting. It's so tiring, Doctor. I just want it to stop. So please, can we stop talking about impossible, unusual, out-of-touch things, and just talk about how to get these women to leave me alone?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: So far, you've described a very specific concern about people in your church and walking club sending you hidden messages. I want to understand this better—do you believe these messages and being watched are happening in a way that could possibly be real, even if others don’t see it, or do you feel like it's something that couldn't possibly happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Doctor, bless your heart, but I just... I feel like a broken record that's been left out in the sun. Of course it could possibly be real! It *is* real! We aren't talking about spaceships or magic tricks or something out of a comic book here. We are talking about everyday, ordinary cruelty from women I thought were my peers. \n\nI don't know how else to explain that I came in here because—like I have said every single time we go over this—even on my better days... and you know, those are the days where the cloud lifts just enough that I might actually have the stamina to run the vacuum over the front rug or maybe boil a couple of eggs for a sandwich without needing to sit in my recliner for an hour afterward... even on those slightly better days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nAnd there is nothing impossible about how they do it. The church secretary, she types up that bulletin every Thursday afternoon on a regular computer in the parish office. It is the easiest thing in the world for them to put little messages about me right in there, right next to the scripture readings and the usher schedule. It's just ink on paper, Doctor. They just open their mouths and disguise it as a comment about the neighborhood or the humidity. \n\nIt is happening right here in real life, in plain English. It's not a hallucination, it's just sneaky behavior. So yes, it is very possible, and it is very real, and I just... I really need us to stop trying to make it sound like a fairy tale and start talking about what I'm supposed to do about these women! Can we please just focus on that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something about yourself or the world that you know is not true or is highly unlikely, even when others have given you clear evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I just... I feel like I am taking crazy pills just sitting here talking to you! We have been in this room for how long now? And you just keep asking me the exact same question, over and over, just using different words you found in a thesaurus. \"Not true?\" \"Highly unlikely?\" \"Evidence to the contrary?\" Good heavens above. I don't know who these \"others\" are that you think are giving me evidence. If you mean Pastor Dave saying he didn't notice anything, well of course he didn't, he's a man and he's busy worrying about the choir and the leaky roof in the fellowship hall! He doesn't see how these women operate.\n\nBut I know what I know. And I know what I see. Like I have said to you until I am absolutely blue in the face... the entire reason I came in to see you today is because even on my better days—and you know what I mean by that now, the days where that awful heaviness lifts just a tiny fraction so I can maybe wipe down the bathroom mirror or water my African violets without needing to go lie down in the dark for the rest of the afternoon—even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. \n\nIt is a constant, nagging, terrible feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. They are watching my every move, and they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, and they are slipping them right into what they say when we're walking together. \n\nThat is the truth! It's not unlikely, it's not untrue, it is happening to me every single week. And no amount of you asking me if I believe in things that aren't real is going to change the fact that these women are doing this to me. I am begging you, Doctor, for the love of all that is holy, can we please just move on from this question? Can we talk about how I'm supposed to handle them? Because I cannot answer this same question a fifth time, I really just can't.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. They are keeping a close eye on everything I do. And they are putting little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin. They print them right there in black and white, tucked between the prayer requests and the potluck announcements. And when we're walking, they slip these little messages right into what they say, disguising them as normal conversation about the weather or their grandchildren. \n\nI know it might sound hard for *you* to believe, because you aren't the one living it. You aren't the one having to read between the lines of every single thing Brenda or Susan says. But it is my reality. It's not out of touch with reality, it *is* my reality, and it's exhausting. It's so tiring, Doctor. I just want it to stop. So please, can we stop talking about impossible, unusual, out-of-touch things, and just talk about how to get these women to leave me alone?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: So far, you've described a very specific concern about people in your church and walking club sending you hidden messages. I want to understand this better—do you believe these messages and being watched are happening in a way that could possibly be real, even if others don’t see it, or do you feel like it's something that couldn't possibly happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Doctor, bless your heart, but I just... I feel like a broken record that's been left out in the sun. Of course it could possibly be real! It *is* real! We aren't talking about spaceships or magic tricks or something out of a comic book here. We are talking about everyday, ordinary cruelty from women I thought were my peers. \n\nI don't know how else to explain that I came in here because—like I have said every single time we go over this—even on my better days... and you know, those are the days where the cloud lifts just enough that I might actually have the stamina to run the vacuum over the front rug or maybe boil a couple of eggs for a sandwich without needing to sit in my recliner for an hour afterward... even on those slightly better days, I just absolutely cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nAnd there is nothing impossible about how they do it. The church secretary, she types up that bulletin every Thursday afternoon on a regular computer in the parish office. It is the easiest thing in the world for them to put little messages about me right in there, right next to the scripture readings and the usher schedule. It's just ink on paper, Doctor. And when we are walking? It doesn't take any kind of impossible fantasy for someone to slip a little message about me into what they say out loud. They just open their mouths and disguise it as a comment about the neighborhood or the humidity. \n\nIt is happening right here in real life, in plain English. It's not a hallucination, it's just sneaky behavior. So yes, it is very possible, and it is very real, and I just... I really need us to stop trying to make it sound like a fairy tale and start talking about what I'm supposed to do about these women! Can we please just focus on that?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something about yourself or the world that you know is not true or is highly unlikely, even when others have given you clear evidence to the contrary?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I just... I feel like I am taking crazy pills just sitting here talking to you! We have been in this room for how long now? And you just keep asking me the exact same question, over and over, just using different words you found in a thesaurus. \"Not true?\" \"Highly unlikely?\" \"Evidence to the contrary?\" Good heavens above. I don't know who these \"others\" are that you think are giving me evidence. If you mean Pastor Dave saying he didn't notice anything, well of course he didn't, he's a man and he's busy worrying about the choir and the leaky roof in the fellowship hall! He doesn't see how these women operate.\n\nBut I know what I know. And I know what I see. Like I have said to you until I am absolutely blue in the face... the entire reason I came in to see you today is because even on my better days—and you know what I mean by that now, the days where that awful heaviness lifts just a tiny fraction so I can maybe wipe down the bathroom mirror or water my African violets without needing to go lie down in the dark for the rest of the afternoon—even on those days, I just cannot shake this feeling. \n\nIt is a constant, nagging, terrible feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. They are watching my every move, and they are putting those little messages about me in the Sunday bulletin, and they are slipping them right into what they say when we're walking together. \n\nThat is the truth! It's not unlikely, it's not untrue, it is happening to me every single week. And no amount of you asking me if I believe in things that aren't real is going to change the fact that these women are doing this to me. I am begging you, Doctor, for the love of all that is holy, can we please just move on from this question? Can we talk about how I'm supposed to handle them? Because I cannot answer this same question a fifth time, I really just can't.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that other people are trying to control your mind, or that you have special abilities or importance that others don't recognize?\n\nPatient: Control my mind? Special abilities? Lord have mercy, Doctor, are you even listening to a single word I am saying to you? I really, truly feel like you have me confused with another patient. I am not a superhero, I am not a psychic, and I certainly don't think I have magical powers or some grand, secret importance. I am just a 66-year-old woman who wants to be left in peace! Mind control? What on earth would make you ask me something like that? I don't have a microchip in my brain, and nobody is trying to steer me around with a remote control. \n\nI just... I don't understand why we are doing this. I came in here—and I am going to say this one more time, because I honestly don't know how else to get through to you—I came in because even on my better days... and by now you should know exactly what I mean by that, the days where I can actually muster up the energy to maybe dust the television stand or put a load of towels in the wash without just sitting on the edge of the bed crying for no reason... even on those better days, I just cannot shake the feeling that some people at church and in the walking club are watching me. \n\nThat is it! That is my problem. They are watching me, and they are putting little messages about me in the bulletin, or slipping them right into what they say when we are walking together. There is no mind control involved. There are no special abilities. Just Brenda, and Susan, and the church secretary making my life an absolute misery with their little messages. \n\nWhy do you keep asking me these bizarre questions? Why can't we just deal with the actual problem I came in here for? I just need to know how to get these women to stop watching me and talking about me in code. That's all I want. Can we please, please just talk about that now?"
  }
